\title{Splitting and the Stickerball Theorem for Taut Fillings\\[2pt]
\large A proof extracted from the Lean formalization}
\author{}
\date{}

\begin{document}
\maketitle

\noindent
This document records, as ordinary mathematics, the two theorems established in the Lean
development \code{lean/Taut}: (II) taut fillings split under almost-disjointness, and
(III) a taut filling of a combinatorial $2$-sphere is an \emph{AnyRootedStickerball}.
The organizing spine is the Lean architecture, not any prior narrative: almost-disjoint
splitting is the engine, and the stickerball theorem is a single induction on the size of
the filling driven by that engine. Typewriter names are the Lean identifiers; their status
is summarized in \S\ref{sec:status}. Everything stated as a theorem or lemma is fully
formalized with no \code{sorry} and no project axioms (\S\ref{sec:status}); the one notion
that is genuinely weaker than its classical namesake, and the one classical bridge that is
\emph{not} proved, are isolated in \S\ref{sec:classical}.

%==========================================================================
\section{Definitions}\label{sec:defs}

Fix a linearly ordered vertex set $V$. A simplex is a finite subset $s\subseteq V$; if
$\card{s}=k+1$ we call it a $k$-simplex. A \emph{chain} is a finitely supported function
$M$ from simplices to $\Z$ (\code{Chain V}), written $M=\sum_s M(s)\,s$, with orientations
fixed by the vertex order. The boundary $\bd$ is the usual signed face operator
(\code{bdry}); $\bd\bd=0$. The \emph{norm} is $\norm{M}=\sum_s\card{M(s)}$ (\code{nrm}); a
chain is \emph{simplicial} if every coefficient lies in $\{-1,0,1\}$
(\code{SimplicialChain}), and then $\norm{M}$ is the number of simplices in its support.

\begin{definition}[Filling volume; taut filling]\label{def:taut}
The \emph{filling volume} of a chain $X$ is $\Zvol(X)=\inf\{\norm{M}:\bd M=X\}$ (\code{Zvol},
literally an $\inf$ over $\mathbb N$). For a cycle ($\bd X=0$) a filling exists, the $\inf$ is
attained, and
\[
\Zvol(X)=\min\{\norm{M}:\bd M=X\}\qquad(\code{exists\_fill\_eq\_Zvol}).
\]
(\code{Zvol} is defined for every chain; it is used below only on cycles.)
A chain $M$ is a \emph{taut filling} of $X$ if $\bd M=X$ and $\norm{M}=\Zvol(X)$, i.e.\ $M$
is a norm-minimal filling of its own boundary (\code{IsTaut}).
\end{definition}

Tautness contributes exactly three things to the proofs, all consequences of minimality:
\begin{itemize}
\item[(T1)] \emph{Boundary control:} $\bd M=X$ holds on the nose.
\item[(T2)] \emph{Purity / no junk:} the generators of a taut filling of an $n$-cycle are
all $(n{+}1)$-simplices (\code{IsTaut.dim\_pure}), and every vertex of $M$ lies on the
boundary (\code{IsTaut.vert\_subset}, via \code{IsTaut.no\_internal\_vertex}).
\item[(T3)] \emph{No extraneous cancellation:} a taut chain contains no \emph{nontrivial}
complete cone $\operatorname{cone} x\,W$ as a subchain --- $W$ closed ($\bd W=0$), the apex $x$
absent from $W$ ($\deg_x W=0$), and some vertex $x'$ present in $W$ ($\deg_{x'} W\neq 0$)
(\code{not\_taut\_complete\_cone}); such a cone could be
refilled with strictly fewer simplices.
\end{itemize}
``Taut'' is therefore purely combinatorial $\ell^1$-minimality with these structural
corollaries; it is not a metric or curvature condition.

\begin{definition}[Combinatorial $2$-sphere]\label{def:sphere}
A set $\sigma$ of triangles is a \emph{combinatorial $2$-sphere} (\code{IsSphere2}) if it is
pure $2$-dimensional, every edge lies in exactly two triangles, every vertex link is
\emph{connected}, the $1$-skeleton is connected, and $V+F=E+2$ (that each vertex link is in fact a
cycle, i.e.\ $2$-regular, is then derived: \code{link\_two\_regular}). These properties are taken
as the definition.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[AlmostDisjoint]\label{def:ad}
Fix $n\ge 1$ and $A,B\subseteq V$. Closed pure $n$-cycles $X,Y$ are
\emph{AlmostDisjoint over $(A,B)$} if every generator of $X$ is an $(n{+}1)$-subset of $A$,
every generator of $Y$ is an $(n{+}1)$-subset of $B$, $\bd X=\bd Y=0$, and
\[
\card{A\cap B}\ \le\ n+1 .
\]
Mathematically: the two regions overlap in at most an $n$-simplex's worth of vertices.
This is the \emph{entire} separation hypothesis --- no metric, no topological transversality.
(Informal name; in Lean it is the hypothesis bundle of
\code{Zvol\_add\_of\_almost\_disjoint\_full} and \code{IsTaut.splits\_full}.)
\end{definition}

For the stickerball theorem we build complexes of tetrahedra one at a time. Let $\tau$ be a
set of tetrahedra ($4$-element simplices) with current boundary $B$ (a set of triangles).

\begin{definition}[Clean glue step; clean shelling]\label{def:clean}
Adding a tetrahedron $t$ to $\tau$ is a \emph{clean glue step} (\code{CleanGlueStep}) if:
\begin{itemize}
\item[(a)] $t$ meets the current boundary $B$ in one or two of its four triangular faces,
and the new boundary is $B$ updated by symmetric difference with the faces of $t$;
\item[(b)] $t\notin\tau$ and no already-interior face of $t$ is rogue (each old face of $t$
lies in a shared boundary triangle); and the three \emph{link certificates}:
\item[(c1)] each triangle of $t$ lies in at most one tetrahedron of $\tau$ (so at most two
after adding $t$);
\item[(c2)] each edge of $t$ is new to $\tau$, or shares an apex with $\tau$'s edge-link there;
\item[(c3)] each vertex of $t$ is new to $\tau$, or shares an apex with $\tau$'s vertex-link there.
\end{itemize}
A \emph{clean shelling} of $\tau$ with boundary $B$ is an ordering $t_1,\dots,t_k$ of $\tau$
in which $t_1$ is a single tetrahedron and each $t_{i+1}$ is added to $\{t_1,\dots,t_i\}$ by
a clean glue step, terminating at boundary $B$ (\code{IsCleanShelling},
\code{CleanShellFrom}).
\end{definition}

The certificates (c1)--(c3) are precisely the hypotheses needed to preserve, across one
insertion, the three conditions defining a \emph{clean $3$-complex}.

\begin{definition}[Clean $3$-complex]\label{def:clean3}
A set $\tau$ of tetrahedra is a \emph{clean $3$-complex} (\code{IsClean3Complex}) if it is
pure ($3$-dimensional), triangle-bounded (every triangle in at most two tetrahedra), and
\emph{normal}: both its edge-links and its vertex-links are connected.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Stickerball; root; RootedStickerball; AnyRootedStickerball]\label{def:sb}
A set $\tau$ of tetrahedra is a \emph{stickerball} with boundary $B$ if it admits a clean
shelling ending at $B$ and $B\neq\varnothing$ (\code{IsStickerball}).
A \emph{root} of a stickerball is a tetrahedron $t\in\tau$ at which some clean shelling can
\emph{begin}. ``Rooted at $t$'' (\emph{RootedStickerball}) is not a standalone Lean predicate; it
names the existence clause for that $t$ inside \code{FreelyCleanShellable}. The stickerball is an
\emph{AnyRootedStickerball} if every tetrahedron of $\tau$ is a root: in Lean,
\code{IsAnyrootedStickerball} $=$ \code{IsStickerball} together with \code{FreelyCleanShellable},
where \code{FreelyCleanShellable} asserts a clean shelling beginning at each $t\in\tau$.
\end{definition}

\begin{remark}
``RootedStickerball'' and ``AnyRootedStickerball'' are stated in Lean-backed terms. The
clean shelling certifies normality (\S\ref{sec:classical}); we do not silently upgrade
``stickerball'' to ``shellable ball'' or ``$3$-ball'' --- the proved bridge and the missing
bridge are stated in \S\ref{sec:classical}.
\end{remark}

%==========================================================================
\section{Splitting under AlmostDisjoint}\label{sec:split}

The engine of the splitting theorem is a small-support triviality (Lemma~\ref{lem:smallsupp})
together with the no-cone property (T3) of taut chains. We also record here, once, the one
homological input --- $H_1=0$ and the cut it yields (Lemma~\ref{lem:cut}); the splitting theorem
does \emph{not} use it (its analogue of closedness is the coefficient-level
\code{bdry\_lk\_eq\_zero}, not the cut), but the sphere-cutting reductions of \S\ref{sec:main} do.

\subsection*{The homological input (used in \S\ref{sec:main})}

\begin{lemma}[$H_1=0$ and the cut]\label{lem:cut}
On a combinatorial $2$-sphere, over $\F_2$, every $1$-cycle is a boundary:
$\operatorname{im}\bd_2=\ker\bd_1$ (\code{range\_bd2\_eq\_ker\_bd1}). Consequently, if
$\gamma$ is a triple of vertices whose three edges lie in $\sigma$ but which is not a face
of $\sigma$, then the $1$-cycle of its edges bounds a set $W$ of faces of $\sigma$
(\code{exists\_cut}).
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The $\F_2$ $2$-cycles are spanned by the all-faces chain (dual-graph connectivity), so
$\dim\operatorname{im}\bd_2=F-1$; the connected $1$-skeleton gives
$\dim\operatorname{im}\bd_1=V-1$, hence $\dim\ker\bd_1=E-(V-1)$, which equals $F-1$ by
$V+F=E+2$. With $\operatorname{im}\bd_2\subseteq\ker\bd_1$ this forces equality. A non-face
triple $\gamma$ has each vertex on exactly two of its edges, so its edge-chain is a
$1$-cycle and therefore a boundary $\bd_2 W$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Small-support triviality]\label{lem:smallsupp}
A closed chain whose generators all have $n+1$ vertices, supported on a set of at most
$n+1$ vertices, is zero: with fewer than $n+1$ vertices there is no $(n{+}1)$-generator at all
(\code{eq\_zero\_of\_supp\_card\_lt}, closedness not needed), and on exactly $n+1$ vertices the
single possible generator is killed by closedness (\code{eq\_zero\_of\_closed\_supp\_card\_eq}).
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
On at most $n+1$ vertices there is at most one $(n{+}1)$-subset, so the chain is $c\cdot s$
for a single simplex; $\bd(c\,s)=0$ forces $c=0$.
\end{proof}

\subsection*{The split}

Fix a common vertex $p\in A\cap B$. There is a norm-non-increasing \emph{kill operator}
$K_{A,p}$ (\code{Projection.lean}, a cone projection toward $p$) with: $K_{A,p}$ fixes every
chain confined to $A$ (\code{Kmap\_eq\_self}); and $K_{A,p}$ annihilates every closed pure
$n$-cycle confined to $B$ (\code{Kmap\_eq\_zero\_of\_closed}), because its image is a closed
pure cycle on $A\cap B$, hence on $\le n+1$ vertices, hence zero by
Lemma~\ref{lem:smallsupp}. Symmetrically for $K_{B,q}$ with $q\in A\cap B$, $q\neq p$.

\begin{theorem}[Additivity]\label{thm:add}
If $X,Y$ are AlmostDisjoint over $(A,B)$ with $n\ge 1$, then
$\Zvol(X+Y)=\Zvol(X)+\Zvol(Y)$ (\code{Zvol\_add\_of\_almost\_disjoint\_full}).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
$(\le)$ The sum of minimal fillings of $X$ and $Y$ is a filling of $X+Y$, so by subadditivity of
the norm, $\Zvol(X{+}Y)\le\norm{M_X}+\norm{M_Y}=\Zvol X+\Zvol Y$ (\code{Zvol\_le}, \code{nrm\_add\_le}).
$(\ge)$ Let $M$ be a minimal (hence taut) filling of $X+Y$, so $\Zvol(X{+}Y)=\norm M$; its
generators are pure $(n{+}2)$-simplices lying in $A\cup B$ (\code{IsTaut.dim\_pure},
\code{IsTaut.vert\_subset}). Each generator $t$ splits as
$(t\cap A\cap B)\sqcup(t\setminus A)\sqcup(t\setminus B)$; as $\card{A\cap B}\le n+1<n+2$,
$t$ cannot avoid both $t\setminus A$ and $t\setminus B$, so $t$ is killed by \emph{at least
one} of $K_{A,p},K_{B,q}$ (\code{Kkills\_or\_Kkills}, an inclusive ``or''). Equivalently the
two retained sets are disjoint --- a generator may be killed by both (retained by neither),
e.g.\ one with $\card{t\setminus A}=\card{t\setminus B}=2$, and that is harmless. With the
per-map bound $\norm{K_{A,p}M}+\norm{M{\restriction}_{\mathrm{kill}_A}}\le\norm M$
(\code{nrm\_Kmap\_add\_killed\_le}) and
$\norm M\le\norm{M{\restriction}_{\mathrm{kill}_A}}+\norm{M{\restriction}_{\mathrm{kill}_B}}$

 succeeded in 0ms:
$\norm M\le\norm{M{\restriction}_{\mathrm{kill}_A}}+\norm{M{\restriction}_{\mathrm{kill}_B}}$
(\code{nrm\_le\_killed\_add\_killed}) this gives $\norm{K_{A,p}M}+\norm{K_{B,q}M}\le\norm{M}$.
Because $K_{A,p}$ fixes the $A$-part of $\bd M$ and kills its closed $B$-part, $K_{A,p}M$ fills
$X$ and $K_{B,q}M$ fills $Y$; thus $\norm{M}\ge\Zvol X+\Zvol Y$. (Exclusivity --- each generator
killed by \emph{exactly} one --- is not used here; it holds only later, for the taut filling in
Theorem~\ref{thm:split}, once \code{no\_double\_kill} rules out double kills.)
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Splitting under AlmostDisjoint]\label{thm:split}
Let $X,Y$ be AlmostDisjoint over $(A,B)$ with $n\ge 2$, and let $M$ be a taut filling of
$X+Y$. Then, writing $M_A=M\!\restriction_{\,\subseteq A}$ and
$M_B=M\!\restriction_{\,\not\subseteq A}$,
\[
M=M_A+M_B,\qquad \bd M_A=X,\qquad \bd M_B=Y,
\]
and $M_A,M_B$ are themselves taut (\code{IsTaut.splits\_full}).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Tautness enters precisely here, turning Theorem~\ref{thm:add} into rigidity. Since $M$ is
taut, $\norm{M}=\Zvol(X+Y)=\Zvol X+\Zvol Y$, while
$\norm{M}\ge\norm{K_{A,p}M}+\norm{K_{B,q}M}\ge\Zvol X+\Zvol Y$. So all inequalities are
equalities, with two consequences.

\emph{No double kill} (\code{no\_double\_kill}): if some generator were retained by neither
operator, the middle sum would be strictly smaller than $\norm M$. Hence every generator is
retained by exactly one side, and $K_{A,p}M,K_{B,q}M$ are \emph{taut} fillings of $X,Y$.

\emph{No straddling simplex} (\code{no\_extreme\_hybrid}). The three-part decomposition
leaves, besides pure generators ($\subseteq A$ or $\subseteq B$), only ``extreme hybrids'':
a single vertex $y_0$ on the far side and none in $A\cap B$. If such hybrids existed,
collect those through a fixed $y_0$; their faces opposite $y_0$ form a nonempty cycle $L$.
That $L$ is closed is \emph{not} the $\F_2$ cut: a coefficient computation shows that $\bd U$
carries no face through $y_0$ (such faces lie in neither $X$ nor $Y$), and \code{bdry\_lk\_eq\_zero}
then converts this into $\bd L=0$. So $M$ would contain the complete cone $y_0\ast L$,
contradicting (T3). This is the only place $n\ge 2$ is used:
the opposite faces need $\ge 2$ vertices for $L$ to be a genuine cycle.

Thus every generator of $M$ is $\subseteq A$ or $\subseteq B$, so the literal restrictions give
$M=M_A+M_B$. The boundary identities $\bd M_A=X$, $\bd M_B=Y$ follow by applying the $K$-maps to
the boundary equation (\code{Kmap\_eq\_self} on the $A$-part, \code{Kmap\_eq\_zero\_of\_closed} on
the closed $B$-part), and the tautness of the pieces from $\norm{M_A}+\norm{M_B}=\norm M$ together
with $\norm{M_A}\ge\Zvol X$, $\norm{M_B}\ge\Zvol Y$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Why exactly AlmostDisjoint]
The proof uses the separation hypothesis only through Lemma~\ref{lem:smallsupp}, which needs
nothing beyond $\card{A\cap B}\le n+1$. There is no appeal to any geometric or topological
transversality; AlmostDisjointness is the exact combinatorial content that makes the cross
terms vanish.
\end{remark}

The rational analogue (\code{Qvol\_add\_of\_almost\_disjoint\_full},
\code{IsQTaut.splits\_full}) follows by clearing denominators, applying
Theorems~\ref{thm:add}--\ref{thm:split} over $\Z$, and rescaling.

%==========================================================================
\section{Taut fillings are AnyRootedStickerballs}\label{sec:main}

We now prove the main theorem by induction on the number of tetrahedra, using
Theorem~\ref{thm:split} (in its $2$-sphere ``cut'' form) to produce the smaller taut
fillings. First, the engine that gives the filling its $3$-dimensional shape.

\begin{theorem}[Taut fillings are pure and triangle-bounded]\label{thm:pm}
Let $\sigma$ be a combinatorial $2$-sphere, $X$ the $\pm1$ cycle on $\sigma$
(\code{UnitOn X $\sigma$}), and $M$ a taut simplicial filling with $\bd M=X$. Then every
generator of $M$ is a tetrahedron and every triangle lies in at most two of them
(\code{taut\_isPseudomanifold}; purity from \code{IsTaut.dim\_pure}).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Purity is (T2): $X$ is $2$-dimensional, so a taut filling is $3$-dimensional.
Triangle-boundedness is a strong induction on the number of tetrahedra, by the trichotomy
used again below. \emph{Base} ($\sigma$ has $\le 4$ vertices): $M$ is a single tetrahedron.
\emph{Degree-$3$ vertex}: cut its star (Lemma~\ref{lem:cut}), apply the inductive
hypothesis to the smaller filling, re-glue. \emph{No degree-$3$ vertex}: take two disjoint
tetrahedra $e,u$ each meeting the boundary in exactly two faces
(\code{aleph\_disjoint\_eligible\_pair}), remove $e$, apply the inductive hypothesis, re-glue;
the key point is the \emph{triangle rule-out} (\code{faceCount\_removeTet\_sharedFace\_eq\_zero}):
a shared face of $e$ cannot reappear after removing $e$, since the disjoint witness $u$ would
otherwise force that triangle's incidence to $3$ in the inductively triangle-bounded filling
$M-u$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Main]\label{thm:anyrooted}
Let $\sigma$ be a combinatorial $2$-sphere, $X$ the $\pm1$ cycle on $\sigma$, and $M$ a taut
simplicial filling with $\bd M=X$. Then the set of tetrahedra of $M$ is an
\emph{AnyRootedStickerball} with boundary $\sigma$
(\code{taut\_filling\_is\_anyrootedStickerball}).
\end{theorem}

By Theorem~\ref{thm:pm} the support consists of tetrahedra; $\sigma\neq\varnothing$ gives
nonempty boundary. The content is that every tetrahedron is a root:

\begin{theorem}\label{thm:freely}
Under the hypotheses of Theorem~\ref{thm:anyrooted}, for every tetrahedron $t_\star$ of $M$
there is a clean shelling of $M$ beginning with $t_\star$ (\code{theorem3\_clean}, via the
induction skeleton \code{theorem3\_core\_clean} with cases \code{base\_free\_clean},
\code{deg3\_step\_clean}, \code{prime\_step\_clean}).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Strong induction on the number $N$ of tetrahedra of $M$. The inductive invariant is exactly
the AnyRooted statement: a clean shelling beginning at \emph{any} prescribed $t_\star$. The
cases are a trichotomy on $\sigma$. In each non-base case the cut (Lemma~\ref{lem:cut})
supplies strictly smaller taut fillings of smaller spheres to which the inductive hypothesis
applies --- by Theorem~\ref{thm:split} itself in the degree-$3$ case, and by a separate
side-decomposition resting on the same cut in the prime case.

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{Base ($\sigma$ has $\le 4$ vertices).} Then $\sigma$ is a tetrahedron
boundary and $M$ is a single tetrahedron $T$; the one-term shelling starts at $T$ and is
vacuously clean.

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{Degree-$3$ vertex $v$.} The link of $v$ is a triangle $\gamma$, and the
star of $v$ is one tetrahedron $T=v\ast\gamma$. Since $\card{\sigma}\ge 5$, $\gamma$ is a
non-face triangle whose edges lie in $\sigma$, so by Lemma~\ref{lem:cut} replacing the three
triangles at $v$ by $\gamma$ yields a $2$-sphere $\sigma'$ with one fewer vertex. Restricting
$M$ to the non-star side is a \emph{direct instance of Theorem~\ref{thm:split}}, with the two
capped vertex-sets as $A,B$ (their overlap is exactly $\gamma$, so $\card{A\cap B}=n{+}1=3$):
the lemma \code{taut\_splits\_for\_capped\_cut} invokes \code{IsTaut.splits} verbatim. It
produces a \emph{taut} filling $M'$ of $\sigma'$ with strictly fewer tetrahedra, so by
induction $M'$ is an AnyRootedStickerball. Two facts let $T$ be glued cleanly: $\gamma$ is a boundary
triangle of $M'$ lying in a \emph{unique} remainder tetrahedron $t_0$ (tautness pins it,
$\text{faceCount}(\gamma)=1$; \code{degree3\_hanchor}), and $v$ is a vertex absent from $M'$
(\code{degree3\_apex\_notMem\_*}), so adjoining $T$ along the single face $\gamma$ satisfies
(a)--(c3) (the apex $v$ is new, so its link certificates are vacuous). Now assemble, given
$t_\star$:
\begin{itemize}
\item if $t_\star\neq T$: take a clean shelling of $M'$ rooted at $t_\star$ (induction) and
append $T$ last;
\item if $t_\star=T$: start with $T$, then a clean shelling of $M'$ rooted at the anchor
$t_0$ (induction), which glues to $T$ along $\gamma$.
\end{itemize}
Either way $M$ has a clean shelling rooted at $t_\star$.

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{No degree-$3$ vertex ($\card{\sigma}\ge 5$).} Pick a tetrahedron $e$ of $M$
with exactly two faces on $\sigma$ (its existence is a count: with no degree-$3$ vertex there
are more boundary triangles than tetrahedra; \code{exists\_eligibleTet}). Let $f_3,f_4$ be its
two interior faces and $f_3\cap f_4$ its \emph{flip edge}. Remove $e$.
\begin{itemize}
\item \emph{Flip edge absent.} Exposing $f_3,f_4$ is a $2$--$2$ flip of the boundary: the new
boundary is again a $2$-sphere $\sigma'$ on the same vertices
(\code{isSphere2\_flipBoundary\_of\_eligible}) and $M-e$ is a taut filling of it with fewer
tetrahedra. By induction $M-e$ is an AnyRootedStickerball; re-gluing $e$ (two shared faces)
is a clean glue step, its shared faces clean by the rule-out of Theorem~\ref{thm:pm}.
\item \emph{Flip edge present.} Then $f_3\cap f_4\in\sigma$, and $\sigma$ separates along this
edge into two $2$-spheres $\sigma_1,\sigma_2$; correspondingly $M-e$ splits along this edge into
two taut sub-fillings $M_1,M_2$ with fewer tetrahedra (\code{flipEdgePresent\_side\_sets},
\code{flipEdgePresent\_side\_algebra}). This is a decomposition of the same filter-by-side shape
as Theorem~\ref{thm:split}, but a \emph{separate} argument: it is built directly on the cut
(Lemma~\ref{lem:cut}/\code{exists\_cut}), not through \code{IsTaut.splits}.
By induction each is an AnyRootedStickerball; concatenate their clean shellings --- rooting
the second at the tetrahedron adjacent to the bridge --- and insert $e$ to bridge them
(\code{glueStep\_bridge\_left}, \code{glueStep\_bridge\_right}).
\end{itemize}
In both sub-cases, choosing the root of the smaller piece(s) appropriately (and, if
$t_\star=e$, starting with $e$) yields a clean shelling of $M$ rooted at $t_\star$.

\medskip
This proves the inductive invariant for $M$, completing the induction.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:anyrooted}]
Theorem~\ref{thm:freely} gives a clean shelling rooted at each tetrahedron; with nonempty
boundary this is the AnyRootedStickerball conclusion. Each re-gluing above is a clean glue
step, so the link certificates (c1)--(c3) hold at every step; the assembled shelling is
therefore a genuine clean shelling and the rooted structure is preserved through the
assembly.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Where each ingredient is used]
The recursion always lands on strictly smaller taut fillings of strictly smaller spheres,
so the induction terminates. Tautness is used (i) to make every cut-restriction again taut,
so the inductive hypothesis applies (Theorem~\ref{thm:split} for the degree-$3$ cut, the
analogous side-decomposition for the prime case); (ii) through
Theorem~\ref{thm:pm} to keep every intermediate complex triangle-bounded, which the prime
rule-out needs; and (iii) to pin boundary triangles into unique tetrahedra, giving the
gluing anchors. The rooted structure is preserved because every assembly step is a
\emph{clean} glue step.
\end{remark}

%==========================================================================
\section{Lean correspondence and status}\label{sec:status}

The development builds successfully; a search of \code{lean/Taut} finds no \code{sorry},
\code{admit}, \code{native\_decide}, \code{axiom}, or \code{unsafe}. Each theorem below
reduces to the three foundational axioms \code{propext}, \code{Classical.choice},
\code{Quot.sound} only.

\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{lll}
\hline
Result here & Lean identifier & Status\\
\hline
Lem~\ref{lem:cut} ($H_1=0$, cut) & \code{range\_bd2\_eq\_ker\_bd1}, \code{exists\_cut} & fully proved\\
Thm~\ref{thm:add} (additivity) & \code{Zvol\_add\_of\_almost\_disjoint\_full} & fully proved\\
Thm~\ref{thm:split} (splitting) & \code{IsTaut.splits\_full} & fully proved\\
Thm~\ref{thm:pm} (pure, triangle-bounded) & \code{taut\_isPseudomanifold} & fully proved\\
Thm~\ref{thm:freely} (rooted at each tet) & \code{theorem3\_clean} & fully proved\\
Thm~\ref{thm:anyrooted} (AnyRootedStickerball) & \code{taut\_filling\_is\_anyrootedStickerball} & fully proved\\
rational splitting/additivity & \code{IsQTaut.splits\_full}, \code{Qvol\_add\_*} & fully proved\\
\hline
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\noindent\emph{Definitions weaker than their classical namesakes.}
\begin{itemize}
\item \emph{Taut} (\code{IsTaut}) is combinatorial $\ell^1$-minimality
(Definition~\ref{def:taut}), not a geometric condition.
\item \emph{Triangle-bounded} (\code{IsPseudomanifold} in the development) means only ``every
triangle in at most two tetrahedra.'' It is \emph{not} a full pseudomanifold; normality
(link-connectedness) is established separately, via the clean shelling (see
\S\ref{sec:classical}). Theorem~\ref{thm:pm} proves the triangle bound only.
\item \emph{Clean shelling} (\code{IsCleanShelling}) carries the link certificates
(c1)--(c3) as data; this is what makes it certify a clean $3$-complex, and is stronger than a
boundary-only shelling.
\end{itemize}

%==========================================================================
\section{Relation to the classical statement; remaining bridges}\label{sec:classical}

The conclusion of Theorem~\ref{thm:anyrooted} is stated in Lean-backed terms
(AnyRootedStickerball). Two questions separate this from a classical ``shellable normal
$3$-ball'' statement.

\paragraph{Proved bridge: AnyRootedStickerball $\Rightarrow$ normal $3$-pseudomanifold.}
The development proves that a stickerball is a clean $3$-complex:
\[
\code{IsStickerball.isClean3Complex}\colon\quad
\text{stickerball}\ \Longrightarrow\ \text{pure}\ \wedge\ \text{triangle-bounded}\ \wedge\
\text{Normal3},
\]
where \code{Normal3} is \emph{edge-link connected and vertex-link connected}
(\code{IsAnyrootedStickerball.isClean3Complex} for the AnyRooted form). This is proved by
iterating the single-insertion lemma \code{clean3Complex\_insert} along the clean shelling;
the three certificates (c1)--(c3) of each glue step supply exactly the triangle-count,
edge-link, and vertex-link preservation (\code{isPseudomanifold\_insert},
\code{edgeLinkConnected\_insert}, \code{vertexLinkConnected\_insert}). Thus pseudomanifoldness
(triangle bound), edge-link connectedness, vertex-link connectedness, and nonempty boundary
are all \emph{Lean-proved} consequences of the conclusion; the corresponding corollaries are
\code{taut\_filling\_is\_clean3Complex} and \code{taut\_filling\_is\_shellable}.

\paragraph{Missing bridge: stickerball $\Rightarrow$ topological/PL $3$-ball.}
The statement ``a clean-shellable normal $3$-pseudomanifold with nonempty boundary is
(PL-)homeomorphic to the $3$-ball'' is the classical shelling theorem of Danaraj--Klee /
Bj\"orner. \emph{No bridge theorem from \code{IsStickerball} to a topological or PL ball
appears in the current development}; it is recorded only as a docstring remark. Any classical
reading of Theorem~\ref{thm:anyrooted} as ``$M$ triangulates a $3$-ball'' must invoke that
external theorem explicitly. In the present, fully formalized terms the conclusion is exactly:
\emph{the tetrahedra of a taut filling of a combinatorial $2$-sphere form an
AnyRootedStickerball --- a clean-shellable normal $3$-pseudomanifold, with boundary the given
sphere, that can be clean-shelled starting from any tetrahedron}.

\paragraph{Hypotheses to carry into a paper statement.}
Chains are simplicial and the boundary cycle is $\pm1$ on $\sigma$
(\code{SimplicialChain}, \code{UnitOn}); splitting (Theorem~\ref{thm:split}) needs dimension
$n\ge 2$ while additivity (Theorem~\ref{thm:add}) needs only $n\ge 1$; AlmostDisjointness is
$\card{A\cap B}\le n+1$; the ambient $V$ is infinite; and \code{IsSphere2} includes the Euler
relation $V+F=E+2$ as part of its definition, from which $H_1=0$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:cut}) is
derived rather than assumed.

\end{document}

    60	  | [],     _ => False

    62	
    63	/-- A *clean ball*: a tet-set admitting a clean shelling (a nodup ordering of its
    64	tets gluing up cleanly to boundary `B`). -/
    65	def IsCleanBall (τ B : Finset (Finset V)) : Prop :=
    66	  ∃ l : List (Finset V), l.toFinset = τ ∧ l.Nodup ∧ IsCleanShelling l B
    67	
    68	/-- *Freely clean shellable*: **any** tet can serve as the head of a clean
    69	shelling.  This is the public free-sticker-ball predicate Theorem 3 exports. -/
    70	def FreelyCleanShellable (τ B : Finset (Finset V)) : Prop :=
    71	  ∀ t ∈ τ, ∃ l : List (Finset V),
    72	    l.head? = some t ∧ l.toFinset = τ ∧ l.Nodup ∧ IsCleanShelling l B
    73	
    74	/-! ## Stickerball predicates (the combinatorial B³ certificate) -/
    75	
    76	/-- A **stickerball**: a shellable clean 3-complex with nonempty boundary.  Concretely `IsCleanBall`
    77	(a clean shelling exists — building *up* by sticking tetrahedra on, the reverse of shucking down) with
    78	a nonempty boundary `B`.  Being an `IsCleanBall` it is in particular an `IsClean3Complex`
    79	(pure, every triangle in ≤ 2 tets, connected vertex *and* edge links) — see `IsStickerball.isClean3Complex`.
    80	
    81	By the standard PL theorem that a shellable normal 3-pseudomanifold with nonempty boundary is a PL
    82	ball, this is the combinatorial certificate for a triangulation of `B³`.  This project proves the
    83	combinatorial certificate; it does **not** formalize that external PL-homeomorphism theorem (see
    84	`notes/future-projects.md`). -/
    85	def IsStickerball (τ B : Finset (Finset V)) : Prop :=
    86	  IsCleanBall τ B ∧ B.Nonempty
    87	
    88	/-- An **anyrooted stickerball** is a stickerball whose shelling may start with any prescribed
    89	tetrahedron (`FreelyCleanShellable`).  "Stickerball" by itself is *not* anyrooted — that is the
    90	strengthened property. -/
    91	def IsAnyrootedStickerball (τ B : Finset (Finset V)) : Prop :=
    92	  IsStickerball τ B ∧ FreelyCleanShellable τ B
    93	
    94	lemma IsAnyrootedStickerball.toStickerball {τ B : Finset (Finset V)}
    95	    (h : IsAnyrootedStickerball τ B) : IsStickerball τ B := h.1
    96	
    97	lemma IsAnyrootedStickerball.freelyCleanShellable {τ B : Finset (Finset V)}
    98	    (h : IsAnyrootedStickerball τ B) : FreelyCleanShellable τ B := h.2

   323	/-! ## Theorem 1, part 1: additivity of Zvol -/
   324	
   325	lemma exists_fill_eq_Zvol {X : Chain V} (W : Chain V) (hW : bdry W = X) :
   326	    ∃ M : Chain V, bdry M = X ∧ nrm M = Zvol X := by
   327	  obtain ⟨M, h1, h2⟩ :=
   328	    Nat.sInf_mem (⟨nrm W, W, hW, rfl⟩ :
   329	      {m | ∃ M : Chain V, bdry M = X ∧ nrm M = m}.Nonempty)
   330	  exact ⟨M, h1, h2⟩
   331	
   332	/-- The mass of `M` is at most the mass killed by `K_{A,p}` plus the mass
   333	killed by `K_{B,q}`, when every generator is killed on at least one side. -/
   334	lemma nrm_le_killed_add_killed {A B : Finset V} {p q : V} {M : Chain V}
   335	    (hkill : ∀ t ∈ M.support, Kkills A p t ∨ Kkills B q t) :
   336	    nrm M ≤ nrm (M.filter (Kkills A p)) + nrm (M.filter (Kkills B q)) := by
   337	  classical
   338	  have hsubA : (M.filter (Kkills A p)).support ⊆ M.support := by
   339	    rw [Finsupp.support_filter]; exact Finset.filter_subset _ _
   340	  have hsubB : (M.filter (Kkills B q)).support ⊆ M.support := by
   341	    rw [Finsupp.support_filter]; exact Finset.filter_subset _ _
   342	  rw [nrm_eq_sum_subset hsubA, nrm_eq_sum_subset hsubB,
   343	    ← Finset.sum_add_distrib, nrm]
   344	  refine Finset.sum_le_sum fun s hs => ?_
   345	  rw [Finsupp.filter_apply, Finsupp.filter_apply]
   346	  rcases hkill s hs with h | h
   347	  · rw [if_pos h]
   348	    omega
   349	  · rw [if_pos h]
   350	    split <;> omega
   351	
   352	/-- **Theorem 1, additivity** (Ellison): for an almost disjoint union
   353	`X + Y` — `X` a closed pure `n`-chain on `A`, `Y` on `B`, with
   354	`(A ∩ B).card ≤ n + 1` witnessed by two distinct shared vertices
   355	`p ≠ q ∈ A ∩ B` — the filling volume adds:
   356	`Zvol (X + Y) = Zvol X + Zvol Y`. -/
   357	theorem Zvol_add_of_almost_disjoint {A B : Finset V} {p q : V} {n : ℕ}
   358	    (hn : 1 ≤ n) (hp : p ∈ A ∩ B) (hq : q ∈ A ∩ B) (hpq : p ≠ q)
   359	    (hC : (A ∩ B).card ≤ n + 1)
   360	    {X Y : Chain V}
   361	    (hX : ∀ s ∈ X.support, s ⊆ A ∧ s.card = n + 1)
   362	    (hY : ∀ s ∈ Y.support, s ⊆ B ∧ s.card = n + 1)
   363	    (hXc : bdry X = 0) (hYc : bdry Y = 0) :
   364	    Zvol (X + Y) = Zvol X + Zvol Y := by
   365	  have hpA : p ∈ A := (Finset.mem_inter.mp hp).1
   366	  have hqB : q ∈ B := (Finset.mem_inter.mp hq).2
   367	  have hXYc : bdry (X + Y) = 0 := by rw [map_add, hXc, hYc, add_zero]
   368	  obtain ⟨Mx, hMx, hMxn⟩ := exists_fill_eq_Zvol (cone p X) (bdry_cone_of_closed hXc)
   369	  obtain ⟨My, hMy, hMyn⟩ := exists_fill_eq_Zvol (cone p Y) (bdry_cone_of_closed hYc)
   370	  obtain ⟨M, hM1, hM2⟩ :=
   371	    exists_fill_eq_Zvol (cone p (X + Y)) (bdry_cone_of_closed hXYc)
   372	  refine le_antisymm ?_ ?_
   373	  · have hbd : bdry (Mx + My) = X + Y := by rw [map_add, hMx, hMy]
   374	    calc Zvol (X + Y) ≤ nrm (Mx + My) := Zvol_le hbd
   375	      _ ≤ nrm Mx + nrm My := nrm_add_le _ _
   376	      _ = Zvol X + Zvol Y := by rw [hMxn, hMyn]
   377	  · have hMt : IsTaut M := by rw [IsTaut, hM1]; exact hM2
   378	    have hbdsupp : ∀ s ∈ (bdry M).support, s.card = n + 1 := by
   379	      intro s hs
   380	      rw [hM1] at hs
   381	      rcases Finset.mem_union.mp (Finsupp.support_add hs) with h | h

    72	/-! ## Zvol and tautness -/
    73	
    74	/-- The filling volume: the least L¹ norm of a chain with boundary `X`. -/
    75	noncomputable def Zvol (X : Chain V) : ℕ :=
    76	  sInf {n | ∃ M : Chain V, bdry M = X ∧ nrm M = n}
    77	
    78	/-- `M` is an optimal filling of its own boundary. -/
    79	def IsTaut (M : Chain V) : Prop := nrm M = Zvol (bdry M)
    80	
    81	lemma Zvol_le {X M : Chain V} (h : bdry M = X) : Zvol X ≤ nrm M :=
    82	  Nat.sInf_le ⟨M, h, rfl⟩
    83	
    84	lemma bdry_cone_of_closed {x : V} {X : Chain V} (hX : bdry X = 0) :
    85	    bdry (cone x X) = X := by
    86	  have h := bdry_cone_add_cone_bdry x X
    87	  rw [hX, map_zero, add_zero] at h
    88	  exact h
    89	
    90	lemma exists_optimal_fill (W : Chain V) :
    91	    ∃ M : Chain V, bdry M = bdry W ∧ nrm M = Zvol (bdry W) := by
    92	  have hne : {n | ∃ M : Chain V, bdry M = bdry W ∧ nrm M = n}.Nonempty :=
    93	    ⟨nrm W, W, rfl, rfl⟩
    94	  obtain ⟨M, hM, hn⟩ := Nat.sInf_mem hne
    95	  exact ⟨M, hM, hn⟩
    96	
    97	/-- Proposition 1 (subtaut): a sub-chain of a taut chain is taut. -/
    98	theorem IsTaut.subChain {M U : Chain V} (hM : IsTaut M) (hU : SubChain U M) :
    99	    IsTaut U := by
   100	  obtain ⟨W, hW, hWn⟩ := exists_optimal_fill U
   101	  have hbd : bdry (M - U + W) = bdry M := by
   102	    rw [map_add, map_sub, hW]
   103	    abel
   104	  have hle : Zvol (bdry M) ≤ nrm (M - U) + nrm W :=
   105	    le_trans (Zvol_le hbd) (nrm_add_le _ _)
   106	  have hsplit := hU.nrm_add_nrm_sub
   107	  have h1 : Zvol (bdry U) ≤ nrm U := Zvol_le rfl
   108	  rw [IsTaut] at hM ⊢
   109	  omega
   110	
   111	/-- Proposition 2 (maxdeg bound, per vertex): for closed `X`,
   112	`Zvol X + deg x X ≤ nrm X`. -/
   113	theorem Zvol_add_deg_le (x : V) {X : Chain V} (hX : bdry X = 0) :
   114	    Zvol X + deg x X ≤ nrm X := by
   115	  have h1 : Zvol X ≤ nrm (cone x X) := Zvol_le (bdry_cone_of_closed hX)
   116	  have h2 := nrm_cone_add_deg x X
   117	  omega
   118	
   119	/-- Proposition 3 (nocone): a taut chain contains no nontrivial complete
   120	cone.  Here `cone x W ⊂ M` with `W` closed, `x ∉ vert W` (i.e.
   121	`deg x W = 0`), and nontriviality is witnessed by a vertex `x'` of `W`. -/
   122	theorem not_taut_complete_cone {x x' : V} {W M : Chain V}
   123	    (hM : IsTaut M) (hU : SubChain (cone x W) M) (hW : bdry W = 0)
   124	    (hx : deg x W = 0) (hx' : deg x' W ≠ 0) : False := by
   125	  have hUt : IsTaut (cone x W) := hM.subChain hU
   126	  have hbd : bdry (cone x W) = W := bdry_cone_of_closed hW
   127	  have h1 := nrm_cone_add_deg x W
   128	  have h2 := Zvol_add_deg_le x' hW
   129	  rw [IsTaut, hbd] at hUt
   130	  omega

   498	/-- **Theorem 1, splitting (full paper statement)**: for `n ≥ 2`, the
   499	hypothesis `(A ∩ B).card ≤ n + 1` alone suffices — no witnessing pair
   500	`p ≠ q` is needed.  When `|A ∩ B| ≥ 2` we extract a pair and invoke
   501	`IsTaut.splits`; when `|A ∩ B| ≤ 1` we enlarge both sides by the same
   502	fresh vertices `F` to reach `|A' ∩ B'| = 2 ≤ n + 1`, run the split along
   503	`· ⊆ A'`, then transfer it back to `· ⊆ A`.  The transfer is valid because
   504	every generator of the filling `M` lies in `A ∪ B`, hence is disjoint from
   505	the fresh set `F`, so `t ⊆ A' ↔ t ⊆ A` on `M.support`.

    88	structure IsClosedSurface (σ : Finset (Finset V)) : Prop where
    89	  pure : ∀ f ∈ σ, f.card = 3
    90	  closed : ∀ e ∈ edgesOf σ, edgeDeg σ e = 2
    91	  linkConn : ∀ v ∈ vertsOf σ, ConnOn (linkGraph σ v) (linkVerts σ v)
    92	  conn : ConnOn (skel σ) (vertsOf σ)
    93	
    94	/-- A combinatorial 2-sphere: a closed surface with Euler characteristic 2.
    95	This is the paper's "clean complex" (normal pseudomanifold) sharpened by

    97	structure IsSphere2 (σ : Finset (Finset V)) : Prop where

   101	  conn : ConnOn (skel σ) (vertsOf σ)
   102	  euler : (vertsOf σ).card + σ.card = (edgesOf σ).card + 2

    26	\title{Splitting and the Stickerball Theorem for Taut Fillings\\[2pt]
    27	\large A proof extracted from the Lean formalization}
    28	\author{}
    29	\date{}
    30	
    31	\begin{document}
    32	\maketitle
    33	
    34	\noindent
    35	This document records, as ordinary mathematics, the two theorems established in the Lean
    36	development \code{lean/Taut}: (II) taut fillings split under almost-disjointness, and
    37	(III) a taut filling of a combinatorial $2$-sphere is an \emph{AnyRootedStickerball}.
    38	The organizing spine is the Lean architecture, not any prior narrative: almost-disjoint
    39	splitting is the engine, and the stickerball theorem is a single induction on the size of
    40	the filling driven by that engine. Typewriter names are the Lean identifiers; their status
    41	is summarized in \S\ref{sec:status}. Everything stated as a theorem or lemma is fully
    42	formalized with no \code{sorry} and no project axioms (\S\ref{sec:status}); the one notion
    43	that is genuinely weaker than its classical namesake, and the one classical bridge that is
    44	\emph{not} proved, are isolated in \S\ref{sec:classical}.
    45	
    46	%==========================================================================
    47	\section{Definitions}\label{sec:defs}
    48	
    49	Fix a linearly ordered vertex set $V$. A simplex is a finite subset $s\subseteq V$; if
    50	$\card{s}=k+1$ we call it a $k$-simplex. A \emph{chain} is a finitely supported function
    51	$M$ from simplices to $\Z$ (\code{Chain V}), written $M=\sum_s M(s)\,s$, with orientations
    52	fixed by the vertex order. The boundary $\bd$ is the usual signed face operator
    53	(\code{bdry}); $\bd\bd=0$. The \emph{norm} is $\norm{M}=\sum_s\card{M(s)}$ (\code{nrm}); a
    54	chain is \emph{simplicial} if every coefficient lies in $\{-1,0,1\}$
    55	(\code{SimplicialChain}), and then $\norm{M}$ is the number of simplices in its support.
    56	
    57	\begin{definition}[Filling volume; taut filling]\label{def:taut}
    58	The \emph{filling volume} of a chain $X$ is $\Zvol(X)=\inf\{\norm{M}:\bd M=X\}$ (\code{Zvol},
    59	literally an $\inf$ over $\mathbb N$). For a cycle ($\bd X=0$) a filling exists, the $\inf$ is
    60	attained, and
    61	\[
    62	\Zvol(X)=\min\{\norm{M}:\bd M=X\}\qquad(\code{exists\_fill\_eq\_Zvol}).
    63	\]
    64	(\code{Zvol} is defined for every chain; it is used below only on cycles.)
    65	A chain $M$ is a \emph{taut filling} of $X$ if $\bd M=X$ and $\norm{M}=\Zvol(X)$, i.e.\ $M$
    66	is a norm-minimal filling of its own boundary (\code{IsTaut}).
    67	\end{definition}
    68	
    69	Tautness contributes exactly three things to the proofs, all consequences of minimality:
    70	\begin{itemize}
    71	\item[(T1)] \emph{Boundary control:} $\bd M=X$ holds on the nose.
    72	\item[(T2)] \emph{Purity / no junk:} the generators of a taut filling of an $n$-cycle are
    73	all $(n{+}1)$-simplices (\code{IsTaut.dim\_pure}), and every vertex of $M$ lies on the
    74	boundary (\code{IsTaut.vert\_subset}, via \code{IsTaut.no\_internal\_vertex}).
    75	\item[(T3)] \emph{No extraneous cancellation:} a taut chain contains no \emph{nontrivial}
    76	complete cone $\operatorname{cone} x\,W$ as a subchain --- $W$ closed ($\bd W=0$), the apex $x$
    77	absent from $W$ ($\deg_x W=0$), and some vertex $x'$ present in $W$ ($\deg_{x'} W\neq 0$)
    78	(\code{not\_taut\_complete\_cone}); such a cone could be
    79	refilled with strictly fewer simplices.
    80	\end{itemize}
    81	``Taut'' is therefore purely combinatorial $\ell^1$-minimality with these structural
    82	corollaries; it is not a metric or curvature condition.
    83	
    84	\begin{definition}[Combinatorial $2$-sphere]\label{def:sphere}
    85	A set $\sigma$ of triangles is a \emph{combinatorial $2$-sphere} (\code{IsSphere2}) if it is
    86	pure $2$-dimensional, every edge lies in exactly two triangles, every vertex link is
    87	\emph{connected}, the $1$-skeleton is connected, and $V+F=E+2$ (that each vertex link is in fact a
    88	cycle, i.e.\ $2$-regular, is then derived: \code{link\_two\_regular}). These properties are taken
    89	as the definition.
    90	\end{definition}
    91	
    92	\begin{definition}[AlmostDisjoint]\label{def:ad}
    93	Fix $n\ge 1$ and $A,B\subseteq V$. Closed pure $n$-cycles $X,Y$ are
    94	\emph{AlmostDisjoint over $(A,B)$} if every generator of $X$ is an $(n{+}1)$-subset of $A$,
    95	every generator of $Y$ is an $(n{+}1)$-subset of $B$, $\bd X=\bd Y=0$, and
    96	\[
    97	\card{A\cap B}\ \le\ n+1 .
    98	\]
    99	Mathematically: the two regions overlap in at most an $n$-simplex's worth of vertices.
   100	This is the \emph{entire} separation hypothesis --- no metric, no topological transversality.
   101	(Informal name; in Lean it is the hypothesis bundle of
   102	\code{Zvol\_add\_of\_almost\_disjoint\_full} and \code{IsTaut.splits\_full}.)
   103	\end{definition}
   104	
   105	For the stickerball theorem we build complexes of tetrahedra one at a time. Let $\tau$ be a
   106	set of tetrahedra ($4$-element simplices) with current boundary $B$ (a set of triangles).
   107	
   108	\begin{definition}[Clean glue step; clean shelling]\label{def:clean}
   109	Adding a tetrahedron $t$ to $\tau$ is a \emph{clean glue step} (\code{CleanGlueStep}) if:
   110	\begin{itemize}
   111	\item[(a)] $t$ meets the current boundary $B$ in one or two of its four triangular faces,
   112	and the new boundary is $B$ updated by symmetric difference with the faces of $t$;
   113	\item[(b)] $t\notin\tau$ and no already-interior face of $t$ is rogue (each old face of $t$
   114	lies in a shared boundary triangle); and the three \emph{link certificates}:
   115	\item[(c1)] each triangle of $t$ lies in at most one tetrahedron of $\tau$ (so at most two
   116	after adding $t$);
   117	\item[(c2)] each edge of $t$ is new to $\tau$, or shares an apex with $\tau$'s edge-link there;
   118	\item[(c3)] each vertex of $t$ is new to $\tau$, or shares an apex with $\tau$'s vertex-link there.
   119	\end{itemize}
   120	A \emph{clean shelling} of $\tau$ with boundary $B$ is an ordering $t_1,\dots,t_k$ of $\tau$
   121	in which $t_1$ is a single tetrahedron and each $t_{i+1}$ is added to $\{t_1,\dots,t_i\}$ by
   122	a clean glue step, terminating at boundary $B$ (\code{IsCleanShelling},
   123	\code{CleanShellFrom}).
   124	\end{definition}
   125	
   126	The certificates (c1)--(c3) are precisely the hypotheses needed to preserve, across one
   127	insertion, the three conditions defining a \emph{clean $3$-complex}.
   128	
   129	\begin{definition}[Clean $3$-complex]\label{def:clean3}
   130	A set $\tau$ of tetrahedra is a \emph{clean $3$-complex} (\code{IsClean3Complex}) if it is
   131	pure ($3$-dimensional), triangle-bounded (every triangle in at most two tetrahedra), and
   132	\emph{normal}: both its edge-links and its vertex-links are connected.
   133	\end{definition}
   134	
   135	\begin{definition}[Stickerball; root; RootedStickerball; AnyRootedStickerball]\label{def:sb}
   136	A set $\tau$ of tetrahedra is a \emph{stickerball} with boundary $B$ if it admits a clean
   137	shelling ending at $B$ and $B\neq\varnothing$ (\code{IsStickerball}).
   138	A \emph{root} of a stickerball is a tetrahedron $t\in\tau$ at which some clean shelling can
   139	\emph{begin}. ``Rooted at $t$'' (\emph{RootedStickerball}) is not a standalone Lean predicate; it
   140	names the existence clause for that $t$ inside \code{FreelyCleanShellable}. The stickerball is an
   141	\emph{AnyRootedStickerball} if every tetrahedron of $\tau$ is a root: in Lean,
   142	\code{IsAnyrootedStickerball} $=$ \code{IsStickerball} together with \code{FreelyCleanShellable},
   143	where \code{FreelyCleanShellable} asserts a clean shelling beginning at each $t\in\tau$.
   144	\end{definition}
   145	
   146	\begin{remark}
   147	``RootedStickerball'' and ``AnyRootedStickerball'' are stated in Lean-backed terms. The
   148	clean shelling certifies normality (\S\ref{sec:classical}); we do not silently upgrade
   149	``stickerball'' to ``shellable ball'' or ``$3$-ball'' --- the proved bridge and the missing
   150	bridge are stated in \S\ref{sec:classical}.
   151	\end{remark}
   152	
   153	%==========================================================================
   154	\section{Splitting under AlmostDisjoint}\label{sec:split}
   155	
   156	The engine of the splitting theorem is a small-support triviality (Lemma~\ref{lem:smallsupp})
   157	together with the no-cone property (T3) of taut chains. We also record here, once, the one
   158	homological input --- $H_1=0$ and the cut it yields (Lemma~\ref{lem:cut}); the splitting theorem
   159	does \emph{not} use it (its analogue of closedness is the coefficient-level
   160	\code{bdry\_lk\_eq\_zero}, not the cut), but the sphere-cutting reductions of \S\ref{sec:main} do.
   161	
   162	\subsection*{The homological input (used in \S\ref{sec:main})}
   163	
   164	\begin{lemma}[$H_1=0$ and the cut]\label{lem:cut}
   165	On a combinatorial $2$-sphere, over $\F_2$, every $1$-cycle is a boundary:
   166	$\operatorname{im}\bd_2=\ker\bd_1$ (\code{range\_bd2\_eq\_ker\_bd1}). Consequently, if
   167	$\gamma$ is a triple of vertices whose three edges lie in $\sigma$ but which is not a face
   168	of $\sigma$, then the $1$-cycle of its edges bounds a set $W$ of faces of $\sigma$
   169	(\code{exists\_cut}).
   170	\end{lemma}
   171	
   172	\begin{proof}
   173	The $\F_2$ $2$-cycles are spanned by the all-faces chain (dual-graph connectivity), so
   174	$\dim\operatorname{im}\bd_2=F-1$; the connected $1$-skeleton gives
   175	$\dim\operatorname{im}\bd_1=V-1$, hence $\dim\ker\bd_1=E-(V-1)$, which equals $F-1$ by
   176	$V+F=E+2$. With $\operatorname{im}\bd_2\subseteq\ker\bd_1$ this forces equality. A non-face
   177	triple $\gamma$ has each vertex on exactly two of its edges, so its edge-chain is a
   178	$1$-cycle and therefore a boundary $\bd_2 W$.
   179	\end{proof}
   180	
   181	\begin{lemma}[Small-support triviality]\label{lem:smallsupp}
   182	A closed chain whose generators all have $n+1$ vertices, supported on a set of at most
   183	$n+1$ vertices, is zero: with fewer than $n+1$ vertices there is no $(n{+}1)$-generator at all
   184	(\code{eq\_zero\_of\_supp\_card\_lt}, closedness not needed), and on exactly $n+1$ vertices the
   185	single possible generator is killed by closedness (\code{eq\_zero\_of\_closed\_supp\_card\_eq}).
   186	\end{lemma}
   187	
   188	\begin{proof}
   189	On at most $n+1$ vertices there is at most one $(n{+}1)$-subset, so the chain is $c\cdot s$
   190	for a single simplex; $\bd(c\,s)=0$ forces $c=0$.
   191	\end{proof}
   192	
   193	\subsection*{The split}
   194	
   195	Fix a common vertex $p\in A\cap B$. There is a norm-non-increasing \emph{kill operator}
   196	$K_{A,p}$ (\code{Projection.lean}, a cone projection toward $p$) with: $K_{A,p}$ fixes every
   197	chain confined to $A$ (\code{Kmap\_eq\_self}); and $K_{A,p}$ annihilates every closed pure
   198	$n$-cycle confined to $B$ (\code{Kmap\_eq\_zero\_of\_closed}), because its image is a closed
   199	pure cycle on $A\cap B$, hence on $\le n+1$ vertices, hence zero by
   200	Lemma~\ref{lem:smallsupp}. Symmetrically for $K_{B,q}$ with $q\in A\cap B$, $q\neq p$.
   201	
   202	\begin{theorem}[Additivity]\label{thm:add}
   203	If $X,Y$ are AlmostDisjoint over $(A,B)$ with $n\ge 1$, then
   204	$\Zvol(X+Y)=\Zvol(X)+\Zvol(Y)$ (\code{Zvol\_add\_of\_almost\_disjoint\_full}).
   205	\end{theorem}
   206	
   207	\begin{proof}
   208	$(\le)$ The sum of minimal fillings of $X$ and $Y$ is a filling of $X+Y$, so by subadditivity of
   209	the norm, $\Zvol(X{+}Y)\le\norm{M_X}+\norm{M_Y}=\Zvol X+\Zvol Y$ (\code{Zvol\_le}, \code{nrm\_add\_le}).
   210	$(\ge)$ Let $M$ be a minimal (hence taut) filling of $X+Y$, so $\Zvol(X{+}Y)=\norm M$; its
   211	generators are pure $(n{+}2)$-simplices lying in $A\cup B$ (\code{IsTaut.dim\_pure},
   212	\code{IsTaut.vert\_subset}). Each generator $t$ splits as
   213	$(t\cap A\cap B)\sqcup(t\setminus A)\sqcup(t\setminus B)$; as $\card{A\cap B}\le n+1<n+2$,
   214	$t$ cannot avoid both $t\setminus A$ and $t\setminus B$, so $t$ is killed by \emph{at least
   215	one} of $K_{A,p},K_{B,q}$ (\code{Kkills\_or\_Kkills}, an inclusive ``or''). Equivalently the
   216	two retained sets are disjoint --- a generator may be killed by both (retained by neither),
   217	e.g.\ one with $\card{t\setminus A}=\card{t\setminus B}=2$, and that is harmless. With the
   218	per-map bound $\norm{K_{A,p}M}+\norm{M{\restriction}_{\mathrm{kill}_A}}\le\norm M$
   219	(\code{nrm\_Kmap\_add\_killed\_le}) and
   220	$\norm M\le\norm{M{\restriction}_{\mathrm{kill}_A}}+\norm{M{\restriction}_{\mathrm{kill}_B}}$
   221	(\code{nrm\_le\_killed\_add\_killed}) this gives $\norm{K_{A,p}M}+\norm{K_{B,q}M}\le\norm{M}$.
   222	Because $K_{A,p}$ fixes the $A$-part of $\bd M$ and kills its closed $B$-part, $K_{A,p}M$ fills
   223	$X$ and $K_{B,q}M$ fills $Y$; thus $\norm{M}\ge\Zvol X+\Zvol Y$. (Exclusivity --- each generator
   224	killed by \emph{exactly} one --- is not used here; it holds only later, for the taut filling in
   225	Theorem~\ref{thm:split}, once \code{no\_double\_kill} rules out double kills.)
   226	\end{proof}
   227	
   228	\begin{theorem}[Splitting under AlmostDisjoint]\label{thm:split}
   229	Let $X,Y$ be AlmostDisjoint over $(A,B)$ with $n\ge 2$, and let $M$ be a taut filling of
   230	$X+Y$. Then, writing $M_A=M\!\restriction_{\,\subseteq A}$ and
   231	$M_B=M\!\restriction_{\,\not\subseteq A}$,
   232	\[
   233	M=M_A+M_B,\qquad \bd M_A=X,\qquad \bd M_B=Y,
   234	\]
   235	and $M_A,M_B$ are themselves taut (\code{IsTaut.splits\_full}).
   236	\end{theorem}
   237	
   238	\begin{proof}
   239	Tautness enters precisely here, turning Theorem~\ref{thm:add} into rigidity. Since $M$ is
   240	taut, $\norm{M}=\Zvol(X+Y)=\Zvol X+\Zvol Y$, while
   241	$\norm{M}\ge\norm{K_{A,p}M}+\norm{K_{B,q}M}\ge\Zvol X+\Zvol Y$. So all inequalities are
   242	equalities, with two consequences.
   243	
   244	\emph{No double kill} (\code{no\_double\_kill}): if some generator were retained by neither
   245	operator, the middle sum would be strictly smaller than $\norm M$. Hence every generator is
   246	retained by exactly one side, and $K_{A,p}M,K_{B,q}M$ are \emph{taut} fillings of $X,Y$.
   247	
   248	\emph{No straddling simplex} (\code{no\_extreme\_hybrid}). The three-part decomposition
   249	leaves, besides pure generators ($\subseteq A$ or $\subseteq B$), only ``extreme hybrids'':
   250	a single vertex $y_0$ on the far side and none in $A\cap B$. If such hybrids existed,
   251	collect those through a fixed $y_0$; their faces opposite $y_0$ form a nonempty cycle $L$.
   252	That $L$ is closed is \emph{not} the $\F_2$ cut: a coefficient computation shows that $\bd U$
   253	carries no face through $y_0$ (such faces lie in neither $X$ nor $Y$), and \code{bdry\_lk\_eq\_zero}
   254	then converts this into $\bd L=0$. So $M$ would contain the complete cone $y_0\ast L$,
   255	contradicting (T3). This is the only place $n\ge 2$ is used:
   256	the opposite faces need $\ge 2$ vertices for $L$ to be a genuine cycle.
   257	
   258	Thus every generator of $M$ is $\subseteq A$ or $\subseteq B$, so the literal restrictions give
   259	$M=M_A+M_B$. The boundary identities $\bd M_A=X$, $\bd M_B=Y$ follow by applying the $K$-maps to
   260	the boundary equation (\code{Kmap\_eq\_self} on the $A$-part, \code{Kmap\_eq\_zero\_of\_closed} on
   261	the closed $B$-part), and the tautness of the pieces from $\norm{M_A}+\norm{M_B}=\norm M$ together
   262	with $\norm{M_A}\ge\Zvol X$, $\norm{M_B}\ge\Zvol Y$.
   263	\end{proof}
   264	
   265	\begin{remark}[Why exactly AlmostDisjoint]
   266	The proof uses the separation hypothesis only through Lemma~\ref{lem:smallsupp}, which needs
   267	nothing beyond $\card{A\cap B}\le n+1$. There is no appeal to any geometric or topological
   268	transversality; AlmostDisjointness is the exact combinatorial content that makes the cross
   269	terms vanish.
   270	\end{remark}
   271	
   272	The rational analogue (\code{Qvol\_add\_of\_almost\_disjoint\_full},
   273	\code{IsQTaut.splits\_full}) follows by clearing denominators, applying
   274	Theorems~\ref{thm:add}--\ref{thm:split} over $\Z$, and rescaling.
   275	
   276	%==========================================================================
   277	\section{Taut fillings are AnyRootedStickerballs}\label{sec:main}
   278	
   279	We now prove the main theorem by induction on the number of tetrahedra, using
   280	Theorem~\ref{thm:split} (in its $2$-sphere ``cut'' form) to produce the smaller taut
   281	fillings. First, the engine that gives the filling its $3$-dimensional shape.
   282	
   283	\begin{theorem}[Taut fillings are pure and triangle-bounded]\label{thm:pm}
   284	Let $\sigma$ be a combinatorial $2$-sphere, $X$ the $\pm1$ cycle on $\sigma$
   285	(\code{UnitOn X $\sigma$}), and $M$ a taut simplicial filling with $\bd M=X$. Then every
   286	generator of $M$ is a tetrahedron and every triangle lies in at most two of them
   287	(\code{taut\_isPseudomanifold}; purity from \code{IsTaut.dim\_pure}).
   288	\end{theorem}
   289	
   290	\begin{proof}
   291	Purity is (T2): $X$ is $2$-dimensional, so a taut filling is $3$-dimensional.
   292	Triangle-boundedness is a strong induction on the number of tetrahedra, by the trichotomy
   293	used again below. \emph{Base} ($\sigma$ has $\le 4$ vertices): $M$ is a single tetrahedron.
   294	\emph{Degree-$3$ vertex}: cut its star (Lemma~\ref{lem:cut}), apply the inductive
   295	hypothesis to the smaller filling, re-glue. \emph{No degree-$3$ vertex}: take two disjoint
   296	tetrahedra $e,u$ each meeting the boundary in exactly two faces
   297	(\code{aleph\_disjoint\_eligible\_pair}), remove $e$, apply the inductive hypothesis, re-glue;
   298	the key point is the \emph{triangle rule-out} (\code{faceCount\_removeTet\_sharedFace\_eq\_zero}):
   299	a shared face of $e$ cannot reappear after removing $e$, since the disjoint witness $u$ would
   300	otherwise force that triangle's incidence to $3$ in the inductively triangle-bounded filling
   301	$M-u$.
   302	\end{proof}
   303	
   304	\begin{theorem}[Main]\label{thm:anyrooted}
   305	Let $\sigma$ be a combinatorial $2$-sphere, $X$ the $\pm1$ cycle on $\sigma$, and $M$ a taut
   306	simplicial filling with $\bd M=X$. Then the set of tetrahedra of $M$ is an
   307	\emph{AnyRootedStickerball} with boundary $\sigma$
   308	(\code{taut\_filling\_is\_anyrootedStickerball}).
   309	\end{theorem}
   310	
   311	By Theorem~\ref{thm:pm} the support consists of tetrahedra; $\sigma\neq\varnothing$ gives
   312	nonempty boundary. The content is that every tetrahedron is a root:
   313	
   314	\begin{theorem}\label{thm:freely}
   315	Under the hypotheses of Theorem~\ref{thm:anyrooted}, for every tetrahedron $t_\star$ of $M$
   316	there is a clean shelling of $M$ beginning with $t_\star$ (\code{theorem3\_clean}, via the
   317	induction skeleton \code{theorem3\_core\_clean} with cases \code{base\_free\_clean},
   318	\code{deg3\_step\_clean}, \code{prime\_step\_clean}).
   319	\end{theorem}
   320	
   321	\begin{proof}
   322	Strong induction on the number $N$ of tetrahedra of $M$. The inductive invariant is exactly
   323	the AnyRooted statement: a clean shelling beginning at \emph{any} prescribed $t_\star$. The
   324	cases are a trichotomy on $\sigma$. In each non-base case the cut (Lemma~\ref{lem:cut})
   325	supplies strictly smaller taut fillings of smaller spheres to which the inductive hypothesis
   326	applies --- by Theorem~\ref{thm:split} itself in the degree-$3$ case, and by a separate
   327	side-decomposition resting on the same cut in the prime case.
   328	
   329	\medskip
   330	\noindent\textbf{Base ($\sigma$ has $\le 4$ vertices).} Then $\sigma$ is a tetrahedron
   331	boundary and $M$ is a single tetrahedron $T$; the one-term shelling starts at $T$ and is
   332	vacuously clean.
   333	
   334	\medskip
   335	\noindent\textbf{Degree-$3$ vertex $v$.} The link of $v$ is a triangle $\gamma$, and the
   336	star of $v$ is one tetrahedron $T=v\ast\gamma$. Since $\card{\sigma}\ge 5$, $\gamma$ is a
   337	non-face triangle whose edges lie in $\sigma$, so by Lemma~\ref{lem:cut} replacing the three
   338	triangles at $v$ by $\gamma$ yields a $2$-sphere $\sigma'$ with one fewer vertex. Restricting
   339	$M$ to the non-star side is a \emph{direct instance of Theorem~\ref{thm:split}}, with the two
   340	capped vertex-sets as $A,B$ (their overlap is exactly $\gamma$, so $\card{A\cap B}=n{+}1=3$):
   341	the lemma \code{taut\_splits\_for\_capped\_cut} invokes \code{IsTaut.splits} verbatim. It
   342	produces a \emph{taut} filling $M'$ of $\sigma'$ with strictly fewer tetrahedra, so by
   343	induction $M'$ is an AnyRootedStickerball. Two facts let $T$ be glued cleanly: $\gamma$ is a boundary
   344	triangle of $M'$ lying in a \emph{unique} remainder tetrahedron $t_0$ (tautness pins it,
   345	$\text{faceCount}(\gamma)=1$; \code{degree3\_hanchor}), and $v$ is a vertex absent from $M'$
   346	(\code{degree3\_apex\_notMem\_*}), so adjoining $T$ along the single face $\gamma$ satisfies
   347	(a)--(c3) (the apex $v$ is new, so its link certificates are vacuous). Now assemble, given
   348	$t_\star$:
   349	\begin{itemize}
   350	\item if $t_\star\neq T$: take a clean shelling of $M'$ rooted at $t_\star$ (induction) and
   351	append $T$ last;
   352	\item if $t_\star=T$: start with $T$, then a clean shelling of $M'$ rooted at the anchor
   353	$t_0$ (induction), which glues to $T$ along $\gamma$.
   354	\end{itemize}
   355	Either way $M$ has a clean shelling rooted at $t_\star$.
   356	
   357	\medskip
   358	\noindent\textbf{No degree-$3$ vertex ($\card{\sigma}\ge 5$).} Pick a tetrahedron $e$ of $M$
   359	with exactly two faces on $\sigma$ (its existence is a count: with no degree-$3$ vertex there
   360	are more boundary triangles than tetrahedra; \code{exists\_eligibleTet}). Let $f_3,f_4$ be its
   361	two interior faces and $f_3\cap f_4$ its \emph{flip edge}. Remove $e$.
   362	\begin{itemize}
   363	\item \emph{Flip edge absent.} Exposing $f_3,f_4$ is a $2$--$2$ flip of the boundary: the new
   364	boundary is again a $2$-sphere $\sigma'$ on the same vertices
   365	(\code{isSphere2\_flipBoundary\_of\_eligible}) and $M-e$ is a taut filling of it with fewer
   366	tetrahedra. By induction $M-e$ is an AnyRootedStickerball; re-gluing $e$ (two shared faces)
   367	is a clean glue step, its shared faces clean by the rule-out of Theorem~\ref{thm:pm}.
   368	\item \emph{Flip edge present.} Then $f_3\cap f_4\in\sigma$, and $\sigma$ separates along this
   369	edge into two $2$-spheres $\sigma_1,\sigma_2$; correspondingly $M-e$ splits along this edge into
   370	two taut sub-fillings $M_1,M_2$ with fewer tetrahedra (\code{flipEdgePresent\_side\_sets},
   371	\code{flipEdgePresent\_side\_algebra}). This is a decomposition of the same filter-by-side shape
   372	as Theorem~\ref{thm:split}, but a \emph{separate} argument: it is built directly on the cut
   373	(Lemma~\ref{lem:cut}/\code{exists\_cut}), not through \code{IsTaut.splits}.
   374	By induction each is an AnyRootedStickerball; concatenate their clean shellings --- rooting
   375	the second at the tetrahedron adjacent to the bridge --- and insert $e$ to bridge them
   376	(\code{glueStep\_bridge\_left}, \code{glueStep\_bridge\_right}).
   377	\end{itemize}
   378	In both sub-cases, choosing the root of the smaller piece(s) appropriately (and, if
   379	$t_\star=e$, starting with $e$) yields a clean shelling of $M$ rooted at $t_\star$.
   380	
   381	\medskip
   382	This proves the inductive invariant for $M$, completing the induction.
   383	\end{proof}
   384	
   385	\begin{proof}[Proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:anyrooted}]
   386	Theorem~\ref{thm:freely} gives a clean shelling rooted at each tetrahedron; with nonempty
   387	boundary this is the AnyRootedStickerball conclusion. Each re-gluing above is a clean glue
   388	step, so the link certificates (c1)--(c3) hold at every step; the assembled shelling is
   389	therefore a genuine clean shelling and the rooted structure is preserved through the
   390	assembly.
   391	\end{proof}
   392	
   393	\begin{remark}[Where each ingredient is used]
   394	The recursion always lands on strictly smaller taut fillings of strictly smaller spheres,
   395	so the induction terminates. Tautness is used (i) to make every cut-restriction again taut,
   396	so the inductive hypothesis applies (Theorem~\ref{thm:split} for the degree-$3$ cut, the
   397	analogous side-decomposition for the prime case); (ii) through
   398	Theorem~\ref{thm:pm} to keep every intermediate complex triangle-bounded, which the prime
   399	rule-out needs; and (iii) to pin boundary triangles into unique tetrahedra, giving the
   400	gluing anchors. The rooted structure is preserved because every assembly step is a
   401	\emph{clean} glue step.
   402	\end{remark}
   403	
   404	%==========================================================================
   405	\section{Lean correspondence and status}\label{sec:status}
   406	
   407	The development builds successfully; a search of \code{lean/Taut} finds no \code{sorry},
   408	\code{admit}, \code{native\_decide}, \code{axiom}, or \code{unsafe}. Each theorem below
   409	reduces to the three foundational axioms \code{propext}, \code{Classical.choice},
   410	\code{Quot.sound} only.
   411	
   412	\begin{center}\small
   413	\begin{tabular}{lll}
   414	\hline
   415	Result here & Lean identifier & Status\\
   416	\hline
   417	Lem~\ref{lem:cut} ($H_1=0$, cut) & \code{range\_bd2\_eq\_ker\_bd1}, \code{exists\_cut} & fully proved\\
   418	Thm~\ref{thm:add} (additivity) & \code{Zvol\_add\_of\_almost\_disjoint\_full} & fully proved\\
   419	Thm~\ref{thm:split} (splitting) & \code{IsTaut.splits\_full} & fully proved\\
   420	Thm~\ref{thm:pm} (pure, triangle-bounded) & \code{taut\_isPseudomanifold} & fully proved\\
   421	Thm~\ref{thm:freely} (rooted at each tet) & \code{theorem3\_clean} & fully proved\\
   422	Thm~\ref{thm:anyrooted} (AnyRootedStickerball) & \code{taut\_filling\_is\_anyrootedStickerball} & fully proved\\
   423	rational splitting/additivity & \code{IsQTaut.splits\_full}, \code{Qvol\_add\_*} & fully proved\\
   424	\hline
   425	\end{tabular}
   426	\end{center}
   427	
   428	\noindent\emph{Definitions weaker than their classical namesakes.}
   429	\begin{itemize}
   430	\item \emph{Taut} (\code{IsTaut}) is combinatorial $\ell^1$-minimality
   431	(Definition~\ref{def:taut}), not a geometric condition.
   432	\item \emph{Triangle-bounded} (\code{IsPseudomanifold} in the development) means only ``every
   433	triangle in at most two tetrahedra.'' It is \emph{not} a full pseudomanifold; normality
   434	(link-connectedness) is established separately, via the clean shelling (see
   435	\S\ref{sec:classical}). Theorem~\ref{thm:pm} proves the triangle bound only.
   436	\item \emph{Clean shelling} (\code{IsCleanShelling}) carries the link certificates
   437	(c1)--(c3) as data; this is what makes it certify a clean $3$-complex, and is stronger than a
   438	boundary-only shelling.
   439	\end{itemize}
   440	
   441	%==========================================================================
   442	\section{Relation to the classical statement; remaining bridges}\label{sec:classical}
   443	
   444	The conclusion of Theorem~\ref{thm:anyrooted} is stated in Lean-backed terms
   445	(AnyRootedStickerball). Two questions separate this from a classical ``shellable normal
   446	$3$-ball'' statement.
   447	
   448	\paragraph{Proved bridge: AnyRootedStickerball $\Rightarrow$ normal $3$-pseudomanifold.}
   449	The development proves that a stickerball is a clean $3$-complex:
   450	\[
   451	\code{IsStickerball.isClean3Complex}\colon\quad
   452	\text{stickerball}\ \Longrightarrow\ \text{pure}\ \wedge\ \text{triangle-bounded}\ \wedge\
   453	\text{Normal3},
   454	\]
   455	where \code{Normal3} is \emph{edge-link connected and vertex-link connected}
   456	(\code{IsAnyrootedStickerball.isClean3Complex} for the AnyRooted form). This is proved by
   457	iterating the single-insertion lemma \code{clean3Complex\_insert} along the clean shelling;
   458	the three certificates (c1)--(c3) of each glue step supply exactly the triangle-count,
   459	edge-link, and vertex-link preservation (\code{isPseudomanifold\_insert},
   460	\code{edgeLinkConnected\_insert}, \code{vertexLinkConnected\_insert}). Thus pseudomanifoldness
   461	(triangle bound), edge-link connectedness, vertex-link connectedness, and nonempty boundary
   462	are all \emph{Lean-proved} consequences of the conclusion; the corresponding corollaries are
   463	\code{taut\_filling\_is\_clean3Complex} and \code{taut\_filling\_is\_shellable}.
   464	
   465	\paragraph{Missing bridge: stickerball $\Rightarrow$ topological/PL $3$-ball.}
   466	The statement ``a clean-shellable normal $3$-pseudomanifold with nonempty boundary is
   467	(PL-)homeomorphic to the $3$-ball'' is the classical shelling theorem of Danaraj--Klee /
   468	Bj\"orner. \emph{No bridge theorem from \code{IsStickerball} to a topological or PL ball
   469	appears in the current development}; it is recorded only as a docstring remark. Any classical
   470	reading of Theorem~\ref{thm:anyrooted} as ``$M$ triangulates a $3$-ball'' must invoke that
   471	external theorem explicitly. In the present, fully formalized terms the conclusion is exactly:
   472	\emph{the tetrahedra of a taut filling of a combinatorial $2$-sphere form an
   473	AnyRootedStickerball --- a clean-shellable normal $3$-pseudomanifold, with boundary the given
   474	sphere, that can be clean-shelled starting from any tetrahedron}.
   475	
   476	\paragraph{Hypotheses to carry into a paper statement.}
   477	Chains are simplicial and the boundary cycle is $\pm1$ on $\sigma$
   478	(\code{SimplicialChain}, \code{UnitOn}); splitting (Theorem~\ref{thm:split}) needs dimension
   479	$n\ge 2$ while additivity (Theorem~\ref{thm:add}) needs only $n\ge 1$; AlmostDisjointness is
   480	$\card{A\cap B}\le n+1$; the ambient $V$ is infinite; and \code{IsSphere2} includes the Euler
   481	relation $V+F=E+2$ as part of its definition, from which $H_1=0$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:cut}) is
   482	derived rather than assumed.
   483	
   484	\end{document}

    20	
    21	/-- Number of tets of `τ` that contain the face `f`. -/
    22	def faceCount (τ : Finset (Finset V)) (f : Finset V) : ℕ :=
    23	  (τ.filter (fun t => f ⊆ t)).card
    24	
    25	/-- `τ` is a 3-pseudomanifold: every triangle lies in at most two tets.
    26	With a unit boundary this forces every boundary triangle into exactly one tet
    27	(two would cancel to `0` or sum to `±2`, never the boundary value `±1`). -/
    28	def IsPseudomanifold (τ : Finset (Finset V)) : Prop :=
    29	  ∀ f, f.card = 3 → faceCount τ f ≤ 2
    30	
    31	/-- `TriangleBounded3` — the triangle-count condition (`IsPseudomanifold`): every
    32	triangle lies in at most two tets.  This is *not* a normal/clean pseudomanifold:
    33	it admits two balls joined along an edge, or along a single vertex. -/
    34	abbrev TriangleBounded3 (τ : Finset (Finset V)) : Prop := IsPseudomanifold τ
    35	
    36	/-- A face contained in no tet has incidence zero — used to discharge the
    37	triangle-cleanliness hypothesis from the triangle rule-out (`s₁,s₂ ∉ M−t`). -/
    38	lemma faceCount_eq_zero {τ : Finset (Finset V)} {f : Finset V}
    39	    (h : ∀ t ∈ τ, ¬ f ⊆ t) : faceCount τ f = 0 := by
    40	  unfold faceCount
    41	  rw [Finset.filter_false_of_mem h]; rfl
    42	
    43	lemma faceCount_insert_of_not_mem {τ : Finset (Finset V)} {t : Finset V}
    44	    (ht : t ∉ τ) (f : Finset V) :
    45	    faceCount (insert t τ) f = faceCount τ f + (if f ⊆ t then 1 else 0) := by
    46	  unfold faceCount
    47	  rw [Finset.filter_insert]
    48	  by_cases h : f ⊆ t
   400	condition is `Clean3Complex`: pure, triangle-bounded, and *normal* (both links
   401	connected).  This is what the public shellability predicate must certify. -/
   402	
   403	/-- `Pure3 τ`: every tet has 4 vertices (genuine 3-dimensionality). -/
   404	def Pure3 (τ : Finset (Finset V)) : Prop := ∀ t ∈ τ, t.card = 4
   405	
   406	/-- `Normal3 τ`: connected edge links *and* connected vertex links — the normal
   407	(no-pinch) condition.  `TriangleBounded3 ∧ EdgeLinkConnected` alone is not normal. -/
   408	def Normal3 (τ : Finset (Finset V)) : Prop :=
   409	  EdgeLinkConnected τ ∧ VertexLinkConnected τ
   410	
   411	/-- `Clean3Complex τ`: pure, triangle-bounded, and normal — the honest
   412	"simplicial triangulation of a 3-ball" invariant the public shelling must carry,
   413	not the boundary-only trace.  Exposed under the `Is`-prefixed names
   414	`IsClean3Complex` / `IsNormal3Psman` as the preferred public vocabulary. -/
   415	def Clean3Complex (τ : Finset (Finset V)) : Prop :=
   416	  Pure3 τ ∧ TriangleBounded3 τ ∧ Normal3 τ
   417	
   418	/-- **`IsClean3Complex`** — the clean (normal) 3-complex condition: pure, every triangle in one or two
   419	tets, and *both* links connected (`VertexLinkConnected` and `EdgeLinkConnected`).  This is the local
   420	combinatorial-manifold condition; preferred public name for `Clean3Complex`. -/
   421	abbrev IsClean3Complex (τ : Finset (Finset V)) : Prop := Clean3Complex τ
   422	
   423	/-- **`IsNormal3Psman`** — synonym for `IsClean3Complex`: a normal 3-pseudomanifold (triangle-bounded
   424	with both links connected, pure). -/
   425	abbrev IsNormal3Psman (τ : Finset (Finset V)) : Prop := Clean3Complex τ
   426	
   427	lemma pure3_singleton {t : Finset V} (ht : t.card = 4) :
   428	    Pure3 ({t} : Finset (Finset V)) := by
   429	  intro s hs; rw [Finset.mem_singleton] at hs; subst hs; exact ht

     4	/-!
     5	# Theorem 2: a taut filling of a triangulated S² is a stickerball B³
     6	
     7	This file builds toward `taut_filling_is_stickerball` by the
     8	minimal-counterexample induction of the paper (§"Filling a triangulation of the
     9	2-sphere").
    10	-/
    11	
    12	namespace Taut
    13	
    14	open Finset
    15	
    16	variable {V : Type*} [LinearOrder V]
    17	
    18	/-- A chain `X` is a *unit chain on `σ`* if it is supported exactly on `σ` with
    19	every coefficient `±1`. The boundary `X(σ)` of a triangulated sphere is such a
    20	chain (some choice of orientation); nothing downstream depends on the choice. -/
    21	def UnitOn (X : Chain V) (σ : Finset (Finset V)) : Prop :=
    22	  X.support = σ ∧ ∀ s ∈ σ, X s = 1 ∨ X s = -1
    23	
    24	/-- A chain is *simplicial* if every coefficient is `-1`, `0`, or `1` — i.e. it
    25	is a genuine multiset of oriented simplices with no repeats. A taut filling that
    26	"arises from a triangulation" is such a chain on the tets of a ball. -/
    27	def SimplicialChain (M : Chain V) : Prop :=
    28	  ∀ t, M t = -1 ∨ M t = 0 ∨ M t = 1
    29	
    30	lemma SimplicialChain.natAbs_eq_one {M : Chain V} (h : SimplicialChain M) {s : Finset V}
    31	    (hs : s ∈ M.support) : (M s).natAbs = 1 := by
    32	  have hne : M s ≠ 0 := Finsupp.mem_support_iff.mp hs
    33	  rcases h s with h1 | h0 | h1
    34	  · rw [h1]; decide
    35	  · exact absurd h0 hne
  1530	/-- **PM-carry: every taut filling of a combinatorial 2-sphere is a
  1531	pseudomanifold.** A standalone strong induction
  1532	carrying `IsPseudomanifold M.support` (each triangle in ≤ 2 tets). Base case
  1533	(≤ 4 vertices) by `base_isPM`; degree-3 vertex by `deg3_isPM`; otherwise by
  1534	`prime_isPM`; the strong induction hypothesis is threaded to both non-base
  1535	steps. -/
  1536	theorem taut_isPseudomanifold {σ : Finset (Finset V)} {X M : Chain V} (hσ : IsSphere2 σ)
  1537	    (hU : UnitOn X σ) (hXc : bdry X = 0) (hMX : bdry M = X) (hT : IsTaut M)
  1538	    (hS : SimplicialChain M) : IsPseudomanifold M.support := by
  1539	  suffices H : ∀ N, ∀ (σ : Finset (Finset V)) (X M : Chain V), nrm M = N → IsSphere2 σ →
  1540	      UnitOn X σ → bdry X = 0 → bdry M = X → IsTaut M → SimplicialChain M →
  1541	      IsPseudomanifold M.support by
  1542	    exact H (nrm M) σ X M rfl hσ hU hXc hMX hT hS
  1543	  intro N
  1544	  induction N using Nat.strong_induction_on with
  1545	  | _ N IH =>
  1546	    intro σ X M hN hσ hU hXc hMX hT hS
  1547	    by_cases hv : (vertsOf σ).card ≤ 4
  1548	    · exact base_isPM σ X M hσ hU hMX hT hS hv
  1549	    · push_neg at hv
  1550	      by_cases hd3 : HasDegree3Vertex σ
  1551	      · refine deg3_isPM σ X M hσ hv hU hXc hMX hT hS hd3 ?_
  1552	        intro σ' X' M' hlt hσ' hU' hX'c hM'X' hT' hS'
  1553	        exact IH (nrm M') (hN ▸ hlt) σ' X' M' rfl hσ' hU' hX'c hM'X' hT' hS'
  1554	      · have hno3 : NoDegree3Vertex σ := fun v hvv hcard => hd3 ⟨v, hvv, hcard⟩
  1555	        refine prime_isPM σ X M hσ hU hXc hMX hT hS hno3 ?_
  1556	        intro σ' X' M' hlt hσ' hU' hX'c hM'X' hT' hS'
  1557	        exact IH (nrm M') (hN ▸ hlt) σ' X' M' rfl hσ' hU' hX'c hM'X' hT' hS'
  1558	
  1559	/-- **Shared faces of the eligible tet straddle the cut.** Each of the two
  1560	boundary-shared faces of `e` contains both the A-apex (`f₃ \ f₄`) and the B-apex
  1561	(`f₄ \ f₃`) of `e`; since the A-apex lies in `A \ B` and the B-apex in `B \ A`
  1562	(the apexes are off the flip edge `A ∩ B = f₃ ∩ f₄`), each shared face is neither
  1563	`⊆ A` nor `⊆ B`. -/
  1564	theorem sharedFaces_straddle {σ : Finset (Finset V)} {X M : Chain V}
  1565	    {e f₃ f₄ A B : Finset V}
  3370	  · exact exists_clean_shelling_prime_case2 hσ hU hXc hMX hT hS IH he hne_es hs hexp hf₃₄ hFlip
  3371	  · exact exists_clean_shelling_prime_case1 hσ hU hXc hMX hT hS IH he hu hdu hne_es hs hexp
  3372	      hf₃₄ hFlip
